5	site loop's branch, never mixed with code/docs.
6	
7	Rule: a commit whose staged set contains BOTH code/docs AND generated files is REFUSED. A pure-code commit and a
8	pure-generated commit (the site loop's own) are both fine. Install as a git pre-commit hook (see INSTALL below);
9	not installed automatically so it never blocks a commit mid-run without the builder choosing to.
10	
11	INSTALL (tonight, non-launch):  printf '#!/bin/sh\\npython scripts/precommit_guard.py || exit 1\\n' > .git/hooks/pre-commit && chmod +x .git/hooks/pre-commit
12	"""
13	from __future__ import annotations
14	
15	import os
16	import subprocess
17	import sys
18	
19	_GEN_EXT = (".json", ".log", ".csv", ".tsv")
20	
21	
22	def is_generated(path):
23	    """A machine-written output, not source. site/** and reports/** data/readouts."""
24	    p = path.replace("\\", "/")
25	    if p.startswith("site/"):
26	        return True
27	    if p.startswith("reports/") and (p.endswith(_GEN_EXT) or "READOUT" in os.path.basename(p).upper()):
28	        return True
29	    return False
30	
31	
32	def check(staged):
33	    """(ok, offenders): ok is False only when the commit MIXES code/docs with generated files."""
34	    gen = [p for p in staged if is_generated(p)]
35	    code = [p for p in staged if not is_generated(p)]
36	    if gen and code:
37	        return False, gen
38	    return True, []
39	
40	
41	def _staged():
42	    out = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only"], capture_output=True, text=True)
43	    return [ln for ln in out.stdout.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
44	
45	
46	def _demo():
47	    ok, off = check(["scripts/a.py", "docs/b.md"])
48	    assert ok and off == []                                             # pure code -> fine
49	    ok, off = check(["site/index.html", "reports/STATUS.json"])
50	    assert ok and off == []                                             # pure generated (site loop) -> fine
51	    ok, off = check(["scripts/a.py", "site/index.html", "reports/x.json"])
52	    assert not ok and set(off) == {"site/index.html", "reports/x.json"}  # mixed -> refused, offenders named
53	    assert is_generated("reports/BOOK20-READOUT.json") and is_generated("site/data/x.json")
54	    assert not is_generated("docs/NEXT-PASTE.md") and not is_generated("reports/notes.md")  # md report kept as code-side
55	    print("precommit_guard demo OK: mixed code+generated refused (offenders named), pure commits pass.")
56	
57	
58	if __name__ == "__main__":
59	    if "--demo" in sys.argv:
60	        _demo()
61	    else:
62	        ok, offenders = check(_staged())
63	        if not ok:
64	            sys.stderr.write("REFUSED: a code commit is sweeping generated files (commit them separately):\n  "
65	                             + "\n  ".join(offenders) + "\n")
66	            sys.exit(1)

145	def _read(p):
146	    try:
147	        return open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
148	    except Exception:
149	        return ""
150	
151	
152	def _suite_evidence():
153	    """R-VERIFIER (owner 23 Sep): the pre-run FULL suite result (run ALONE under the RAM gate), with a sha256 so
154	    the report pins exactly which result was trusted. The verifier reads this instead of re-running the ~21-min
155	    suite (which cannot finish inside a single-shot cap). Returns (summary_line_with_hash, raw_json) or ('','')."""
156	    import hashlib
157	    import json as _json
158	    try:
159	        raw = open(SUITE_RESULT, "rb").read()
160	        h = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
161	        d = _json.loads(raw.decode("utf-8"))
162	        line = ("pre-run FULL suite (run ALONE under the RAM gate) sha256=%s: status=%s, %s passed, %s failed, "
163	                "min_free=%s GB, peak_used=%s GB" % (h, d.get("status"), d.get("passed"), d.get("failed"),
164	                                                     d.get("min_free_gb"), d.get("peak_used_gb")))
165	        return line, raw.decode("utf-8", "replace")
166	    except Exception:
167	        return "", ""

tool_result: 95	def verify(commit, ruling_text="", claimed_step="", brain_fn=None, post_fn=None):
96	    """Run the verifier for one commit. brain_fn(inputs)->{'verdict','evidence'} does the actual exam; default
97	    wires to the active rung. Writes docs/reports/verify-<commit>.md and posts one line. Returns the verdict."""
98	    rung, name, avail = active_rung()
99	    post_fn = post_fn or _post
100	    if not avail:
101	        _write_report(commit, "UNAVAILABLE", rung, name, claimed_step, "no brain rung available")
102	        post_fn("VERIFY UNAVAILABLE %s %s" % (claimed_step, commit))
103	        return "UNAVAILABLE"
104	    inputs = {"commit": commit, "ruling": ruling_text, "step": claimed_step,
105	              "diff": _diff(commit), "template": _read(os.path.join(REPO, "docs", "VERIFIER-TEMPLATE.md")),
106	              "map": _read(os.path.join(REPO, "docs", "VERIFY-MAP.md"))}
107	    brain_fn = brain_fn or (lambda i: _run_rung(rung, i))
108	    try:
109	        res = brain_fn(inputs) or {}
110	    except Exception as e:
111	        res = {"verdict": "FAIL", "evidence": "verifier brain raised: %r" % e}
112	    verdict = str(res.get("verdict", "FAIL")).upper()
113	    if verdict not in ("PASS", "FAIL"):
114	        verdict = "FAIL"
115	    _write_report(commit, verdict, rung, name, claimed_step, res.get("evidence", ""))
116	    post_fn("VERIFY %s %s %s" % (verdict, claimed_step, commit))
117	    if verdict == "FAIL":
118	        try:                            # NB21/NB24: a verifier FAIL is a lead mistake — logs a row + posts one 🚩 line
119	            from scripts import lead_mistakes
120	            lead_mistakes.append("step %s reported done at %s" % (claimed_step or "(unspecified)", commit),
121	                                 "independent verifier returned FAIL", notify_fn=post_fn)
122	        except Exception:
123	            pass
124	    return verdict

The correct fix is a **SessionEnd hook that clears the marker on real exit** (safe — it fires on end, not idle, so no double-spawn). Building it:
tool_use Write {"file_path": "[redacted]\\Desktop\\trading-engines-v2\\.claude\\hooks\\session_end.py", "content": "\"\"\"SessionEnd hook (CLAUDE.md \u00a714.1 + \u00a723.3) \u2014 the exit half of the single-instance marker.\n\nThe watchdog's auto-continue (session_resurrect._session_active) treats a session as LIVE while\nscheduler/.owner_active is fresh (< 4h). The SessionStart hook writes it; nothing cleared it on exit, so after a\nreal session end the marker stayed \"fresh\" for up to 4h and the watchdog never reopened (the NB32/NB33 gap). This\nhook CLEARS the marker when the session actually ends, so the watchdog can reopen the next item (RAM + pending +\nnot-login permitting). It fires on a real SessionEnd, never on an idle turn, so a live-but-idle session is never\ndouble-spawned. Fail-open: any error is swallowed.\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport sys\n\nREPO = os.environ.get(\"E1_REPO\") or r\"[redacted]\\Desktop\\trading-engines-v2\"\n\n\ndef clear_marker(path=None):\n    p = path or os.path.join(REPO, \"scheduler\", \".owner_active\")\n    try:\n        if os.path.exists(p):\n            os.remove(p)\n        return True\n    except Exception:\n        return False\n\n\ndef main():\n    try:\n        sys.stdin.read()          # consume the hook payload (reason etc.); we clear on any real end\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    clear_marker()\n    sys.exit(0)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"}

## NB33 — ruling on item 4 + the reopen proof

**RULE FIRST (hash-check) — red → green.** A verifier PASS is now valid **only** if the saved test-suite result carries the commit hash of the *exact* code being checked **and** is green. A missing, different, or red hash turns a PASS into a FAIL — never "trusted." I proved the RED on the real artifact: last night's suite result had no commit stamp, so the check refused it; with the stamp it matches. TDD green (match / mismatch / missing / red-suite), and the downgrade is proven. The suite runner now records which commit it ran on. (commits `455d2b626`, `0702fba03`)

**Item 4 self-test — verdict + 🚩 (cited).** Deleted the stale `item14-broken-test-demo` (it was 363 files out of date). **ARMED — yes.** `Desktop\E1-DESK\VERIFIER-ARMED` placed under your 23-Sep delegation, *after* the self-test proved the verifier catches a broken commit. **RAM numbers (measured, not typed):** stack up ≈ **0.55–1.16 GB** free; stack stopped → ≈ **1.9–2.48 GB**; the reopen floor is **2.0 GB**. With your ChatGPT app (~1.16 GB) + the stack running, free RAM does not clear 2.0 GB — the structural reason the auto-continue reopen has never fired here.

**Reopen proof — the fix + the blocker.** I found *why* it never reopens: the watchdog's "one session at a time" guard reads a marker that **nothing cleared on exit**, so after a session ends it looked "alive" for 4 hours. I added a **SessionEnd hook** that clears it on real exit (fires on end, not idle — no double-spawn). 2	
3	_Append-only. New entries are added at the bottom with a date; nothing above a date is rewritten.
4	Chapter 7 of the project book (`scripts/build_project_book.py` includes this file verbatim). Paper only,
5	RECORD-ONLY — no engine here places a real-money order._
6	
7	Each engine is one page: **what it hunts · why it would earn · data · locks · account · what it sends the
8	owner · state today.** "State today" is the honest state on the disk, not the plan.
9	
10	**ARMING RULE — data its gates need (NB15 item 54, 2026-09-23).** No engine is armed until its **Data** line
11	names, for every gate it reads, the **on-disk store** that gate is computed from (§13 four-part standard:
12	metric · on-disk data source · numeric threshold · action). The pre-open check (MASTER-CHECKLIST item 53)
13	posts **NOT READY** at 08:15 if any armed engine's instrument has no recorded data — so a "Data" line that
14	names a store which does not yet hold that instrument's data keeps the engine unarmed. This is the same field
15	the ruling template now requires (field 9); an engine and the ruling that arms it must agree.
16	
17	---
18	
19	## 2026-09-21 — the catalogue as it stands (R-DAY1-BUNDLE A9)
20	
21	### E1 — price, same-stock (the one live engine)
22	- **What it hunts:** certified same-stock directional patterns (a metric in an extreme band → the next
23	  20-day move is bigger/smaller than usual), each with a walk-forward Wilson lower-bound hit-rate.
24	- **Why it would earn:** at the 20-day horizon the required-move rule clears cost on the mid fill —
25	  +15.45R at limit-at-mid, +1.88R crossing the spread (937 calls, 69.1% win). The edge is essentially the
26	  spread saved by resting at the mid; the live paper book is measuring whether those mid fills are real.
27	- **Data:** the frozen 210-name universe, daily bars + read-only Kite quote/depth at fire time.
28	- **Locks:** the full fail-closed stack — R-HOLD-AND-FIX hard hold, the two-key gate, the per-stock moment
29	  light (its own measured spread ceiling, A1), the 20-position cap, the daily-loss book guard, the circuit
30	  breaker, the kill switch. Any one shut = no order.
31	- **Account:** its own persistent ₹2,00,000 paper account (1R = ₹1,000), run twice — a FLAT reference book
32	  and an ADAPTIVE book — on the same fires.
33	- **Sends the owner:** one fire card at fire time ([E1], PLACED/HELD), a close card on exit, the 30-min
34	  portfolio card (change-gated), the recorders line on login.
35	- **State today:** LIVE paper — **HELD** (R-HOLD-AND-FIX flag present; two-key gate open). Records the
36	  would-be book; places nothing until the owner deletes the hold flag.
37	
38	### E10 — the facts/weights route (now an engine, A4)
39	- **What it hunts:** the same E1 fires, re-sized by pattern FAMILY weight (hit-margin × non-overlap
40	  persistence, shrunk and DD-capped, from prior fires only).
41	- **Why it would earn:** if better families deserve more size. Measured to date it does **not** — weighted
42	  is worse than flat (hit-margin ≠ profitability); it runs so the live comparison is honest, not because it
43	  wins on the sealed data.
44	- **Data:** E1's fires + the armed e10 weights shadow (`roof/store/e10_weights_shadow.json`).
45	- **Locks:** identical to E1 (same order path, same fail-closed stack). Cold-start = flat.
46	- **Account:** own persistent ₹2,00,000 paper account; each fire sized weight(family) × ₹1,000.
47	- **Sends the owner:** its own fire card ([E10]) with its size; the roof copies its executed decision.
48	- **State today:** LIVE paper — HELD. Sizes flat on any family with no measured weight.
49	
50	### E11 — the family-bias layer, NORMAL (now an engine, A4)
51	- **What it hunts:** the same E1 fires, re-sized by the NORMAL family bias (better family → larger). Its
52	  INVERSE, season and expiry-week variants stay **diaries inside E11**, not paper orders.
53	- **Why it would earn:** the mirror hypothesis to E10. Also loses to flat on the sealed data; run for the
54	  live comparison.
55	- **Data:** E1's fires + the armed e11 shadow (`roof/store/e11_family_bias_shadow.json`, `latest_normal`).
56	- **Locks / account:** as E10 — own ₹2,00,000, weight(family) × ₹1,000, cold-start flat.
57	- **Sends the owner:** its own fire card ([E11]); roof copies it.
58	- **State today:** LIVE paper — HELD.
59	
60	### E8 — the roof (COPYCAT, not a peer engine)
61	- **What it hunts:** nothing of its own. It **copies each engine's executed decision** into one desk account
62	  at the same fill, holds N× when N engines make the same trade, cancels opposite trades on one instrument,
63	  and holds the single system-wide risk budget.
64	- **Why it exists:** to net disagreeing engines and cap total risk — the hard prerequisite for any
65	  multi-engine live trading.
66	- **Account:** the desk = ₹2,00,000 × live engines = **₹6,00,000** today (E1, E10, E11).
67	- **State today:** LIVE paper — copies via the engine event log/replay; source ids are engine-distinct so
68	  three engines on one fire never collide. HELD like everything else.
69	
70	### E3 events · E4 options · E7 arbitrage · E12 NSE/BSE · E13 depth · E14 positioning
71	- **E3 (events):** dividends run-up NO EDGE; post-ex drift LOSES; expiry DATA-THIN; earnings/index
72	  incl-excl DATA-WALLED. No event edge survives cost. Diary + daily calendar puller.
73	- **E4 (options):** HAR forecast improves accuracy, but with an honest band the rich-IV signal fires 0
74	  trades on NIFTY/BankNifty — UNDECIDED; single-stock option data is the named remaining test. Recorder-only.
75	- **E7 (arbitrage/parity):** 5 of 8 equations run, none pays at retail (parity FAILS on cost), 3
76	  data-walled. Tick recorder-only.
77	- **E12 (NSE/BSE cross-exchange):** recorder-only (both exchanges' top-of-book).
78	- **E13 (five-level depth):** recorder-only — feeds the per-stock spread ceiling (A1). Read-only.
79	- **E14 (positioning):** recorder-only.
80	- **State today:** all RECORD-ONLY; none places a paper order.
81

86	- **Analyst Scoreboard** — *hunts:* the track record of named market analysts/tipsters (call → outcome).

89	- **YouTube Engine** — two lanes: **My YouTube** (the owner's own subscriptions/creators) and **Whole

92	  paper only.
93	- **Claude Word Analyst** — two variants: **Words** (language-only) and **Words+Numbers** (language plus
94	  the quoted figures). *Hunts:* an edge in how text (announcements, commentary) maps to moves. *State
95	  today:* on paper only.
96	- **World Ledger** — *hunts:* a broad cross-asset/cross-market ledger of what is happening and what it
97	  moved. *State today:* on paper only.

## NB34 — measured reopen floor + master green

**1) Measured reopen floor — the typed 2.0 GB is retired.** A reopen runs the `claude` binary; I measured the live builder's `claude.exe` peak memory at **0.42 GB**, and added the verify's own **0.3 GB** margin → the reopen floor is now **0.72 GB** (`REOPEN_FLOOR_GB = 0.42 + 0.3`, no typed number — each part is a measurement or a cited margin). `session_resurrect` uses it instead of the old 2.0 gate. TDD green (derived-not-typed, gate flips at the floor, unreadable-RAM fails closed); recorded in VERIFIER-BRAIN.md. (commit `af613a91a`)

**Reopen decision — PROVEN (safely, no second session spawned).** At today's free RAM (1.25 GB) the new gate says **yes** (`_ram_ok` = True) where the old 2.0 gate said **no**. Simulating the session as exited, the watchdog logic **launches** and would resume pending item 5. So the floor was the historical blocker, and it's gone.

**Did the reopen fire live? No — with a clear, reduced cause.** The watchdog reopens only after a session's marker is cleared on exit (the NB33 SessionEnd hook does that) **and** RAM ≥ 0.72 (now usually met). 230	        r = subprocess.run([CODEX, "exec", "--sandbox", "workspace-write", "-"], input=prompt,
231	                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=VERIFY_CAP, cwd=CLONE,
232	                           creationflags=0x08000000)
233	        out = (r.stdout or "") + (("\n" + r.stderr) if r.stderr else "")
234	        return out if out.strip() else None
235	    except Exception:
236	        return None
237	
238	
239	def _live_fingerprint(root=None):
240	    """A cheap tamper fingerprint (HEAD + porcelain-status hash) to PROVE the live repo is untouched around a
241	    confined Codex run. None if git cannot be read."""
242	    import hashlib
243	    root = root or REPO
244	    try:
245	        head = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", root, "rev-parse", "HEAD"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip()
246	        status = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", root, "status", "--porcelain"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
247	        return head + ":" + hashlib.sha256(status.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
248	    except Exception:
249	        return None

tool_result: 269	def _run_rung(rung, inputs):
270	    """Production brain. rung (a) = Codex (codex.cmd exec); rung (d) = fresh independent Claude (supervisor
271	    mechanism); (a) falls back to (d) on any Codex failure. Both are handed the pre-run FULL suite result +
272	    its hash and told NOT to re-run the ~21-min suite (it cannot finish in a single shot); they run only the
273	    fast TARGETED checks (module demo + touched-module tests) and confirm the suite hash."""
274	    import sys as _sys
275	    py = _sys.executable                                  # the real interpreter; the 'py' launcher is NOT installed
276	    suite_line, suite_blob = _suite_evidence()
277	    prompt = ("You are the INDEPENDENT VERIFIER. Follow docs/VERIFIER-TEMPLATE.md and run ONLY the TARGETED checks "
278	              "in docs/VERIFY-MAP.md for the touched paths. Run commands with THIS exact Python interpreter (the "
279	              "'py' launcher is NOT installed on this machine): %s\n"
280	              "Targeted checks to run and quote the output of (the -B avoids .pyc writes under a read-only sandbox):\n"
281	              "  1) \"%s\" -B -m engine1_rebuild.conform\n"
282	              "  2) \"%s\" -B -m pytest tests/test_conform.py -q -p no:cacheprovider\n"
283	              "DO NOT re-run the full ~21-min suite: its result was produced ALONE under the RAM gate and is given "
284	              "below as authoritative evidence — confirm the sha256 matches reports/CONFORM-SUITE-RESULT.json on "
285	              "disk. Do NOT modify, stage, or commit any file; run read-only checks only. A claim without a "
286	              "running-evidence line is FAIL. Reply with a first line 'VERDICT: PASS' or 'VERDICT: FAIL' then the "
287	              "evidence.\n\nPRE-RUN SUITE: %s\n%s\n\nRULING:\n%s\n\nCLAIMED STEP: %s\n\nCOMMIT %s DIFF:\n%s\n" %
288	              (py, py, py, suite_line, suite_blob[:1500], inputs["ruling"][:6000], inputs["step"], inputs["commit"],
289	               inputs["diff"]))
290	    out = None
291	    if rung == "a":
292	        cx = _run_codex(prompt)
293	        # Trust a Codex PASS, or a Codex FAIL where the checks ACTUALLY RAN. Fall back to rung (d) only when
294	        # Codex produced nothing OR its sandbox could not execute the checks (owner ruling: rung (d) fallback).
295	        if cx is not None and (re.search(r"(?im)^\s*VERDICT:\s*PASS\b", cx) or not _codex_blocked(cx)):
296	            out = cx
297	    if out is None:
298	        from scripts import supervisor
299	        out, _usage = supervisor._invoke_claude(prompt, timeout=VERIFY_CAP)   # (text, usage) -> unpack
300	    out = out or ""
301	    verdict = "PASS" if re.search(r"(?im)^\s*VERDICT:\s*PASS\b", out) else "FAIL"
302	    evidence = ((suite_line + "\n\n") if suite_line else "") + out            # pin the suite hash into the report
303	    return {"verdict": verdict, "evidence": evidence[:6000]}

def on_row(row, build_fn=None):
    """If `row` is a PLACED/CLOSED row, rebuild status + live-desk. Returns what it did."""
    if not is_trigger(row):
        return {"published": False, "reason": "not a PLACED/CLOSED row"}
    (build_fn or _build)()
    return {"published": True, "status": str(row.get("status")).strip().upper()}


def _demo():
    calls = []
    assert on_row({"status": "PLACED"}, build_fn=lambda: calls.append(1))["published"] is True
    assert on_row({"status": "closed"}, build_fn=lambda: calls.append(1))["published"] is True   # case-insensitive
    assert on_row({"status": "open"}, build_fn=lambda: calls.append(1))["published"] is False     # not a trigger
    assert on_row({}, build_fn=lambda: calls.append(1))["published"] is False
    assert len(calls) == 2, calls        # built only for the two triggers
    print("publish_event demo OK: rebuild fires on PLACED/CLOSED only, case-insensitive, no other status.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import sys
    if "--demo" in sys.argv:
        _demo()

61	def _daily_digest_due():
62	    """R-E1-LEAD-DIGEST: regenerate the lead digest once a day at/after 15:40 IST (the site loop's

73	def once(full=False):
74	    """SITE-CYCLE split: a LIGHT cycle (~every 2 min) publishes only the live data (positions, accounts, cards,
75	    ticker) so the Live desk is current within 2 min; a FULL page rebuild runs ~every 10 min. Both publish to
76	    Pages after building."""
77	    from scripts import site_build, publish_site, lead_digest, snapshot_sync
78	    if full:
79	        site_build.build()                    # full page rebuild (~35 s: results/engine/deliverables/v2/stocks)
80	        _daily_digest_due()                   # keeps the 15:40 one-per-day marker; digest builds each full cycle
81	        lead_digest.build()                   # E1-STATUS-3: refresh the digest so it is never stale
82	        _telegram_day_reports()               # R-E1-TELEGRAM-STYLE A4: 09:00 pre-open, 15:40 day summary
83	    else:
84	        site_build.build_live()               # live data only (~5 s): the Live desk tab's feed + accounts
85	    publish_site.publish(f"E1 live site {_ist():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} IST")   # public Pages, after every build
86	    if not full:
87	        return None                           # light cycles skip the heavier snapshot repo push
88	    # R-RESTART-RESUME (3): the repo commit happens ONLY in the separate snapshot folder (the builder's tree is
89	    # only read), and it PAUSES while a build/audit session holds the repo. The site build and the public-Pages
90	    # publish above keep running — they touch no git lock in this repo (Pages pushes from its own clone).
91	    # Also carries the evening ledger + the scan's pattern board. Push failure: backoff, then next cycle.
92	    return snapshot_sync.sync(f"E1 site refresh {_ist():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} IST") == "pushed"

102	def _start_status_daemon(interval=120):
103	    import threading
104	    def loop():
105	        while True:
106	            try:
107	                _status_tick()
108	            except Exception:
109	                pass
110	            time.sleep(interval)

tool_result: 1	"""NB12 item 2 (item 7 PREPARE half) — apply a verifier PASS that is already on disk, with no live session:
2	stamp a proof marker, queue the orders, post one Telegram line. Idempotent. NEW, un-wired.
3	Wire-in tonight = a watchdog call `proof_apply.apply(commit)`.
4	
5	The three side effects (queue the orders, and — beyond the applied-set marker this module owns — any launch-path
6	proof stamp; and the Telegram post) are INJECTED so nothing launch-path is wired here and the logic is tested with
7	fakes. Idempotency is this module's own: an applied commit is recorded in reports/PROOF-APPLIED.json and a repeat
8	call is a no-op. The PASS check reuses verifier.has_pass (the existing gate helper).
9	"""

19	def _applied_set(path):
20	    try:
21	        return set(json.load(open(path, encoding="utf-8")))
22	    except Exception:
23	        return set()
24	
25	
26	def _record(commit, path):
27	    s = _applied_set(path)
28	    s.add(commit)
29	    json.dump(sorted(s), open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8"), indent=1)

32	def _default_post(line):
33	    import notify                       # lazy: tests never reach the real sender
34	    notify.notify(line)
35

37	def apply(commit, queue_orders_fn, post_fn=None, has_pass_fn=None, marker_fn=None, path=None):
38	    """Apply the verify-PASS for `commit`. `queue_orders_fn(commit)` places the orders (supplied at wire-in).
39	    Returns what it did. Idempotent: a commit already in the applied-set is a no-op."""
40	    path = path or APPLIED
41	    has_pass_fn = has_pass_fn or _has_pass
42	    if not has_pass_fn(commit):
43	        return {"applied": False, "reason": "no verify-PASS on disk for %s" % commit}
44	    if commit in _applied_set(path):
45	        return {"applied": False, "reason": "already applied (idempotent)"}
46	    if marker_fn:
47	        marker_fn(commit)               # optional launch-path proof stamp, supplied at wire-in
48	    queue_orders_fn(commit)             # required: place the orders
49	    (post_fn or _default_post)("PROOF applied — orders queued for %s" % commit)
50	    _record(commit, path)
51	    return {"applied": True, "commit": commit}
52	
53	
54	def _has_pass(commit):
55	    import verifier
56	    return verifier.has_pass(commit)
57

59	def _demo():
60	    import tempfile
61	    p = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), "applied.json")
62	    posts, queued = [], []
63	    q = lambda c: queued.append(c)
64	    # no PASS on disk -> refuse
65	    assert apply("deadbeef", q, post_fn=posts.append, has_pass_fn=lambda c: False, path=p)["applied"] is False
66	    assert queued == [] and posts == []
67	    # PASS present -> applies once
68	    r = apply("cafe123", q, post_fn=posts.append, has_pass_fn=lambda c: True, path=p)
69	    assert r["applied"] is True and queued == ["cafe123"] and len(posts) == 1
70	    # second call -> idempotent no-op (no re-queue, no re-post)
71	    r2 = apply("cafe123", q, post_fn=posts.append, has_pass_fn=lambda c: True, path=p)
72	    assert r2["applied"] is False and queued == ["cafe123"] and len(posts) == 1
73	    print("proof_apply demo OK: refuses without PASS, queues+posts once, idempotent on repeat.")

tool_result: 100	def queue_orders(asof=None, reader=None, quotes=None, write=True):
101	    """Evaluate at the close and, for each QUEUE decision, write a next-open (EOD-) order that the EXISTING
102	    tested path (evening.process_eod_positions -> paper.simulate_trade at the next open) fills with the exam's
103	    own management (template EXAM_TEMPLATE + the pattern's horizon; manage=trail — all five are trail=false).
104	    HELD signals are logged with the named check, never as a trade. Returns {queued, held, decisions}.
105	
106	    GATED on the R-EXAM-CONFORM proof marker: no order is written until conformance is proven (the fail-safe)."""
107	    from engine1_rebuild import book20_runner as B, paper
108	    decisions = evaluate_at_close(asof=asof, reader=reader, quotes=quotes)
109	    proven = os.path.exists(B.EXAM_CONFORM_PROOF)
110	    asof_date = str(asof)[:10] if asof is not None else _today_str()
111	    rows, held = [], []
112	    for d in decisions:
113	        if d["verdict"] == "QUEUE" and proven:
114	            p = next((x for x in live_patterns() if x["stock"] == d["stock"] and x["family"] == d["family"]), None)
115	            if not p or not p.get("candidate"):
116	                continue
117	            hz = p["horizon"]
118	            rows.append({
119	                "trade_id": "EOD-CONFORM-%s-%s" % (d["stock"], asof_date), "status": "queued",
120	                "stock": p["candidate"]["token"], "direction": p["candidate"]["direction"],
121	                "template": EXAM_TEMPLATE, "queue_date": asof_date,
122	                "pattern": "%s %s (exam-conform) next %s days" % (d["stock"], d["family"], hz),
123	            })
124	        elif d["verdict"] == "HELD":
125	            held.append(d)
126	    if write and rows:
127	        paper.append_rows(rows)
128	    return {"queued": len(rows), "held": len(held), "proven": proven, "decisions": decisions}

131	def _checklist(LB, token, entry, quotes, bar_date=None):
132	    """The six checks with LIVE inputs where present, else fail-closed. `quotes` carries the live book; a missing
133	    book -> the check that needs it is HELD (named). Reuses LB.checklist (no copy)."""
134	    try:
135	        # exit_price unknown pre-fill: use entry (a zero-move is 'tradeable' unless data is missing); the cost/
136	        # spread/depth checks are what gate a live fill, and they read the stock's own measures + live book.
137	        chk = LB.checklist(int(token), entry, entry, bar_date or _today_str(), *_impact_k(LB),
138	                           spread_scale=1.0, with_impact=True)
139	        return chk if isinstance(chk, dict) else {"verdict": "HELD", "reason": "warm-up — checklist unavailable"}
